In particular, the last paragraph seems to lack preceding context.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify">clarify the section</a>. There might be a discussion about this on <a href="/wiki/Talk:Uzbekistan" title="Talk:Uzbekistan">the talk page</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2018</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Karimov's first presidential term was extended to 2000 via a <a href="/wiki/1995_Uzbek_presidential_term_referendum" title="1995 Uzbek presidential term referendum">referendum</a>, and he was re-elected in <a href="/wiki/2000_Uzbekistani_presidential_election" class="mw-redirect" title="2000 Uzbekistani presidential election">2000</a>, <a href="/wiki/2007_Uzbekistani_presidential_election" class="mw-redirect" title="2007 Uzbekistani presidential election">2007</a>, and 2015, each time receiving over 90% of the vote. Most international observers refused to participate in the process and did not recognise the results, dismissing them as not meeting basic standards.
</p><p>The 2002 referendum also included a plan for a bicameral parliament consisting of a lower house (the Oliy Majlis) and an upper house (Senate). Members of the lower house are to be "full-time" legislators. Elections for the new bicameral parliament took place on 26 December.
</p><p>Following Islam Karimov's death on 2 September 2016, the <a href="/wiki/Oliy_Majlis" title="Oliy Majlis">Oliy Majlis</a> appointed Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Shavkat_Mirziyoyev" title="Shavkat Mirziyoyev">Shavkat Mirziyoyev</a> as interim president.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Although the chairman of the Senate, <a href="/wiki/Nigmatilla_Yuldashev" title="Nigmatilla Yuldashev">Nigmatilla Yuldashev</a>, was constitutionally designated as Karimov's successor, Yuldashev proposed that Mirziyoyev take the post of the interim president instead in light of Mirziyoyev's "many years of experience". Mirziyoyev was subsequently elected as the country's second president in the <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistani_presidential_election,_2016" class="mw-redirect" title="Uzbekistani presidential election, 2016">December 2016 presidential election</a>, winning 88.6% of the vote, and was sworn in on 14 December.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Deputy Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Abdulla_Aripov" title="Abdulla Aripov">Abdulla Aripov</a> replaced him as prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mirziyoyev removed most of Karimov's officials and urged the government to employ "new, young people who love their country." After a year in office, Mirziyoyev moved away from many of his predecessor's policies. He visited all the Uzbek regions and big cities to get acquainted with the implementation of the projects and reforms which he ordered. Many analysts and Western media compared his rule with <a href="/wiki/Chinese_Communist_Party" title="Chinese Communist Party">Chinese Communist Party</a> leader <a href="/wiki/Deng_Xiaoping" title="Deng Xiaoping">Deng Xiaoping</a> or <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Communist_Party" class="mw-redirect" title="Soviet Communist Party">Soviet Communist Party</a> general secretary <a href="/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev" title="Mikhail Gorbachev">Mikhail Gorbachev</a>. His rule has been quoted as being an "Uzbek Spring".<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Uzbekistan" title="Foreign relations of Uzbekistan">Foreign relations of Uzbekistan</a> and <a href="/wiki/International_organization_membership_of_Uzbekistan" title="International organization membership of Uzbekistan">International organization membership of Uzbekistan</a></div>
<p>Uzbekistan joined the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a> in December 1991. However, it is opposed to reintegration and withdrew from the CIS collective security arrangement in 1999. Since that time, Uzbekistan has participated in the CIS peacekeeping force in Tajikistan and in UN-organized groups to help resolve the Tajikistan and Afghanistan conflicts, both of which it sees as posing threats to its own stability.
</p><p>Previously close to Washington (which gave Uzbekistan half a billion dollars in aid in 2004, about a quarter of its military budget), the government of Uzbekistan has recently restricted American military use of the airbase at <a href="/wiki/Karshi-Khanabad" class="mw-redirect" title="Karshi-Khanabad">Karshi-Khanabad</a> for air operations in neighbouring Afghanistan.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Uzbekistan was an active supporter of U.S. efforts against worldwide terrorism.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The relationship between Uzbekistan and the United States began to deteriorate after the so-called "<a href="/wiki/Colour_revolutions" class="mw-redirect" title="Colour revolutions">colour revolutions</a>" in <a href="/wiki/Georgia_(country)" title="Georgia (country)">Georgia</a> and Ukraine (and to a lesser extent <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan" title="Kyrgyzstan">Kyrgyzstan</a>). When the U.S. joined in a call for an independent international investigation of the bloody events at <a href="/wiki/Andijan_massacre" title="Andijan massacre">Andijan</a>, the relationship further declined, and President Islam Karimov changed the political alignment of the country to bring it closer to Russia and China.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_(22052330394).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_%2822052330394%29.jpg/220px-Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_%2822052330394%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_%2822052330394%29.jpg/330px-Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_%2822052330394%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_%2822052330394%29.jpg/440px-Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_%2822052330394%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4863" data-file-height="3242" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Secretary_Kerry_Meets_With_President_Karimov_at_the_President%27s_Residential_Compound_in_Samarkand_(22052330394).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>President <a href="/wiki/Islam_Karimov" title="Islam Karimov">Islam Karimov</a> with U.S. Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/John_Kerry" title="John Kerry">John Kerry</a> in Samarkand in November 2015</div></div></div>
<p>In late July 2005, the government of Uzbekistan ordered the United States to vacate an airbase in Karshi-Kanabad (near Uzbekistan's border with Afghanistan) within 180 days.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Karimov had offered use of the base to the U.S. shortly after <a href="/wiki/9/11" class="mw-redirect" title="9/11">9/11</a>. It is also believed by some Uzbeks that the protests in Andijan were brought about by the UK and U.S. influences in the area of Andijan.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> This is another reason for the hostility between Uzbekistan and the West.
</p><p>Uzbekistan is a member of the United Nations (UN) (since 2 March 1992), the <a href="/wiki/Euro-Atlantic_Partnership_Council" title="Euro-Atlantic Partnership Council">Euro-Atlantic Partnership Council</a> (EAPC), <a href="/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace" title="Partnership for Peace">Partnership for Peace</a> (PfP), and the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a> (OSCE). It belongs to the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Islamic_Cooperation" title="Organisation of Islamic Cooperation">Organisation of Islamic Cooperation</a> (OIC) and the <a href="/wiki/Economic_Cooperation_Organization" title="Economic Cooperation Organization">Economic Cooperation Organization</a> (ECO) (comprising the five Central Asian countries, <a href="/wiki/Azerbaijan" title="Azerbaijan">Azerbaijan</a>, Iran, Turkey, Afghanistan, and Pakistan). In 1999, Uzbekistan joined the <a href="/wiki/GUAM" class="mw-redirect" title="GUAM">GUAM</a> alliance (Georgia, Ukraine, Azerbaijan and <a href="/wiki/Moldova" title="Moldova">Moldova</a>), which was formed in 1997 (making it GUUAM), but pulled out of the organisation in 2005.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg/220px-2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg/330px-2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg/440px-2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1880" data-file-height="1160" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2015_Summit_of_the_Shanghai_Cooperation_Organization_03.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Leaders present at the <a href="/wiki/Shanghai_Cooperation_Organisation" title="Shanghai Cooperation Organisation">SCO</a> summit in <a href="/wiki/Ufa" title="Ufa">Ufa</a>, Russia in 2015</div></div></div>
<p>Uzbekistan is also a member of the <a href="/wiki/Shanghai_Cooperation_Organisation" title="Shanghai Cooperation Organisation">Shanghai Cooperation Organisation</a> (SCO) and hosts the SCO's Regional Anti-Terrorist Structure (RATS) in Tashkent. Uzbekistan joined the new <a href="/wiki/Central_Asian_Cooperation_Organisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Central Asian Cooperation Organisation">Central Asian Cooperation Organisation</a> (CACO) in 2002. The CACO consists of Uzbekistan, <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>, and Kyrgyzstan. It is a founding member of, and remains involved in, the <a href="/wiki/Central_Asian_Union" title="Central Asian Union">Central Asian Union</a>, formed with Kazakhstan and Kyrgyzstan, and joined in March 1998 by Tajikistan.
</p><p>In December 1994 Uzbekistan applied for the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> membership and received an observer status to start the accession process. The Working Party on the Accession of Uzbekistan to the WTO held its fourth meeting on 7 July 2020 — almost 15 years after its last formal meeting.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2006, <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> presented Islam Karimov an award for Uzbekistan's preservation of its rich culture and traditions.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Despite criticism, this seems to be a sign of improving relationships between Uzbekistan and the West.
</p><p>The month of October 2006 also saw a decrease in the isolation of Uzbekistan from the West. The <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">EU</a> announced that it was planning to send a delegation to Uzbekistan to talk about human rights and liberties, after a long period of hostile relations between the two. Although it is equivocal about whether the official or unofficial version of the <a href="/wiki/Andijan_Massacre" class="mw-redirect" title="Andijan Massacre">Andijan Massacre</a> is true, the EU is evidently willing to ease its economic sanctions against Uzbekistan. Nevertheless, it is generally assumed among Uzbekistan's population that the government will stand firm in maintaining its close ties with the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Federation" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian Federation">Russian Federation</a> and in its theory that the 2004–2005 protests in Uzbekistan were promoted by the US and UK.
</p><p>In January 2008, <a href="/wiki/Lola_Karimova-Tillyaeva" title="Lola Karimova-Tillyaeva">Lola Karimova-Tillyaeva</a> was appointed to her current role as Uzbekistan's ambassador to <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>. Karimova-Tillyaeva and her team have been instrumental in promoting inter-cultural dialogue by increasing European society's awareness of Uzbekistan's cultural and historical heritage.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Human rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Uzbekistan" title="Human rights in Uzbekistan">Human rights in Uzbekistan</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/2005_Andijan_unrest" class="mw-redirect" title="2005 Andijan unrest">2005 Andijan unrest</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Non-governmental_organization" title="Non-governmental organization">Non-governmental</a> human rights organisations, such as <a href="/wiki/International_Helsinki_Federation_for_Human_Rights" title="International Helsinki Federation for Human Rights">IHF</a>, <a href="/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch">Human Rights Watch</a>, <a href="/wiki/Amnesty_International" title="Amnesty International">Amnesty International</a>, as well as <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">United States Department of State</a> and <a href="/wiki/Council_of_the_European_Union" title="Council of the European Union">Council of the European Union</a>, define Uzbekistan as "an authoritarian state with limited civil rights"<sup id="cite_ref-US_State_Dept_-_human_rights_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-US_State_Dept_-_human_rights-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> and express profound concern about "wide-scale violation of virtually all basic human rights".<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
According to the reports, the most widespread violations are <a href="/wiki/Torture" title="Torture">torture</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arbitrary_arrest_and_detention" title="Arbitrary arrest and detention">arbitrary arrests</a>, and various restrictions of freedoms: of religion, of speech and press, of free association and assembly. It has also been reported that forced sterilisation of rural Uzbek women has been sanctioned by the government.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
The reports maintain that the violations are most often committed against members of religious organisations, independent journalists, human rights activists and political activists, including members of the banned opposition parties. As of 2015, reports on violations on human rights in Uzbekistan indicated that violations were still going on without any improvement.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Freedom_House" title="Freedom House">Freedom House</a> has consistently ranked Uzbekistan near the bottom of its Freedom in the World ranking since the country's founding in 1991. In the 2018 report, Uzbekistan was one of the 11 worst countries for <a href="/wiki/Political_Rights" class="mw-redirect" title="Political Rights">Political Rights</a> and <a href="/wiki/Civil_Liberties" class="mw-redirect" title="Civil Liberties">Civil Liberties</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/2005_civil_unrest_in_Uzbekistan" class="mw-redirect" title="2005 civil unrest in Uzbekistan">2005 civil unrest in Uzbekistan</a>, which resulted in several hundred people being killed, is viewed by many as a landmark event in the history of human rights abuse in Uzbekistan.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
Concern has been expressed and requests for an independent investigation of the events has been made by the United States,<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> the European Union,<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> the OSCE Chairman-in-Office and the OSCE Office for Democratic Institutions and Human Rights.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government of Uzbekistan is accused of unlawful termination of human life and of denying its citizens <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_assembly" title="Freedom of assembly">freedom of assembly</a> and freedom of expression. The government vehemently rebuffs the accusations, maintaining that it merely conducted an anti-terrorist operation, exercising only necessary force.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> In addition, some officials claim that "an <a href="/wiki/Information_warfare" title="Information warfare">information war</a> on Uzbekistan has been declared" and the human rights violations in Andijan are invented by the enemies of Uzbekistan as a convenient pretext for intervention in the country's internal affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Male <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Uzbekistan" title="LGBT rights in Uzbekistan">homosexuality</a> is illegal in Uzbekistan.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Punishment ranges from a fine to 3 years in prison.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are an estimated 1.2 million modern slaves in Uzbekistan,<sup id="cite_ref-globalslaveryindex1_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-globalslaveryindex1-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> most work in the cotton industry. The government allegedly forces state employees to pick cotton in the autumn months.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> World Bank loans have been connected to projects that use child labour and forced labour practices in the cotton industry.<sup id="cite_ref-wbloans_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wbloans-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Recent_developments">Recent developments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Recent developments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Islam Karimov died in 2016 and his successor Shavkat Mirziyoyev is considered by most to be pursuing a less autocratic path by increasing co-operation with human rights NGOs,<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> scheduling Soviet-style <a href="/wiki/Exit_visa" class="mw-redirect" title="Exit visa">exit visas</a> to be abolished in 2019,<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> and reducing sentences for certain misdemeanor offences.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Amnesty International report on the country for 2017–2018 found some remnant repressive measures and lack of rule of law in eradicating modern slavery.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> In February 2020, the United Nations announced that Uzbekistan made "major progress" on stamping out forced labour in its cotton harvest as 94% of pickers worked voluntarily.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_Uzbekistan" title="Regions of Uzbekistan">Regions of Uzbekistan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Uzbekistan" title="Districts of Uzbekistan">Districts of Uzbekistan</a></div>
<p>Uzbekistan is divided into twelve <a href="/wiki/Region" title="Region">regions</a> (<i>viloyatlar</i>, singular <i><a href="/wiki/Viloyat" class="mw-redirect" title="Viloyat">viloyat</a></i>, compound noun <i>viloyati</i> e.g., Toshkent <i>viloyati</i>, Samarqand <i>viloyati</i>, etc.), one <a href="/wiki/Autonomous_republic" title="Autonomous republic">autonomous republic</a> (<i>respublika</i>, compound noun <i>respublikasi</i> e.g. Qoraqalpog&#x2bb;iston Muxtor <i>Respublikasi</i>, Karakalpakstan <i>Autonomous Republic</i>, etc.), and one <a href="/wiki/Independent_city" title="Independent city">independent city</a> (<i>shahar</i>, compound noun <i>shahri</i>, e.g., Toshkent <i>shahri</i>). Names are given below in <a href="/wiki/Uzbek_language" title="Uzbek language">Uzbek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Karakalpak_language" title="Karakalpak language">Karakalpak</a> languages when applicable, although numerous variations of the transliterations of each name exist.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:352px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Uzbekistan_provinces.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Uzbekistan_provinces.png/350px-Uzbekistan_provinces.png" decoding="async" width="350" height="228" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/bf/Uzbekistan_provinces.png 1.5x" data-file-width="380" data-file-height="248" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Uzbekistan_provinces.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Political Map of Uzbekistan</div></div></div>

<p>The regions are further divided into <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Uzbekistan" title="Districts of Uzbekistan">districts</a> (<i>tuman</i>).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Largest_cities">Largest cities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Largest cities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div>
<table style="font-size:88%;margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; width: 100%; border: 1px solid darkgray; background: #f9f9f9">

</th></tr>

<td rowspan="11" style="text-align: center"><a href="/wiki/File:International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg" class="image" title="Tashkent"><img alt="Tashkent" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg/120px-International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg" decoding="async" width="120" height="51" class="thumbborder" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg/180px-International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg/240px-International_Business_Center._Tashkent_city.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="439" /></a><br /><a href="/wiki/Tashkent" title="Tashkent">Tashkent</a><br /><a href="/wiki/File:Namangan_Airport.jpg" class="image" title="Namangan"><img alt="Namangan" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Namangan_Airport.jpg/120px-Namangan_Airport.jpg" decoding="async" width="120" height="78" class="thumbborder" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Namangan_Airport.jpg/180px-Namangan_Airport.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Namangan_Airport.jpg/240px-Namangan_Airport.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="663" /></a><br /><a href="/wiki/Namangan" title="Namangan">Namangan</a>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Uzbekistan" title="Economy of Uzbekistan">Economy of Uzbekistan</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Uzbekistan_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Uzbekistan exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg/220px-Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg/330px-Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg/440px-Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Real_GDP_per_capita_development_of_Uzbekistan.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Development of real GDP per capita</div></div></div>
<p>Uzbekistan mines 80 tons of gold annually, seventh in the world. Uzbekistan's copper deposits rank tenth in the world and its uranium deposits twelfth. The country's uranium production ranks seventh globally.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> The Uzbek national gas company, <a href="/wiki/Uzbekneftegas" class="mw-redirect" title="Uzbekneftegas">Uzbekneftegas</a>, ranks 11th in the world in natural gas production with an annual output of 60&#160;to 70&#160;billion cubic metres (2.1–2.5&#160;trillion cubic feet). The country has significant untapped reserves of oil and gas: there are 194 deposits of hydrocarbons in Uzbekistan, including 98 condensate and natural gas deposits and 96 gas condensate deposits.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Uzbekistan improved marginally in the <i>2020 Ease of Doing Business</i> ranking by the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-edbwb2020_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-edbwb2020-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> 
The largest corporations involved in Uzbekistan's energy sector are the <a href="/wiki/China_National_Petroleum_Corporation" title="China National Petroleum Corporation">China National Petroleum Corporation</a> (CNPC), <a href="/wiki/Petronas" title="Petronas">Petronas</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Korea_National_Oil_Corporation" title="Korea National Oil Corporation">Korea National Oil Corporation</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gazprom" title="Gazprom">Gazprom</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lukoil" title="Lukoil">Lukoil</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Uzbekneftegas" class="mw-redirect" title="Uzbekneftegas">Uzbekneftegas</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Along with many <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a> or CIS economies, Uzbekistan's economy declined during the first years of transition and then recovered after 1995, as the cumulative effect of policy reforms began to be felt.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> It has shown robust growth, rising by 4% per year between 1998 and 2003 and accelerating thereafter to 7%–8% per year. According to IMF estimates,<sup id="cite_ref-imf_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> the GDP in 2008 will be almost double its value in 1995 (in constant prices). Since 2003 annual inflation rates varied, reaching almost 40% in 2010 and less than 20% in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Uzbekistan has GNI per capita of US$2,020 in current dollars in 2018, giving a <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">PPP</a> equivalent of US$7,230.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Economic production is concentrated in commodities. In 2011, Uzbekistan was the world's seventh-largest producer and fifth-largest exporter of <a href="/wiki/Cotton" title="Cotton">cotton</a><sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> as well as the seventh-largest world producer of gold. It is also a regionally significant producer of natural gas, coal, copper, oil, silver and uranium.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Agriculture_in_Uzbekistan" title="Agriculture in Uzbekistan">Agriculture</a> employs 27% of Uzbekistan's labour force and contributes 17.4% of its GDP (2012 data).<sup id="cite_ref-uzstat_51-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-uzstat-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Cultivable land is 4.4 million hectares, or about 10% of Uzbekistan's total area. While official unemployment is very low, underemployment – especially in rural areas – is estimated to be at least 20%.<sup id="cite_ref-cia1_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia1-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cotton_production_in_Uzbekistan" title="Cotton production in Uzbekistan">Cotton production in Uzbekistan</a> is important to the national economy of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-guardian_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guardian-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Uzbek cotton is even used to make banknotes in South Korea.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> The country has a considerable production of carrots as well. The use of child labour in Uzbekistan has led several companies, including Tesco,<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> C&amp;A,<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Marks &amp; Spencer, Gap, and H&amp;M, to boycott Uzbek cotton.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_(5982822980).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_%285982822980%29.jpg/220px-Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_%285982822980%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_%285982822980%29.jpg/330px-Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_%285982822980%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_%285982822980%29.jpg/440px-Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_%285982822980%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4416" data-file-height="3312" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Yodgorlik_Silk_Factory_(5982822980).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Yodgorlik <a href="/wiki/Silk" title="Silk">silk</a> factory</div></div></div>
<p>Facing a multitude of economic challenges upon acquiring independence, the government adopted an evolutionary reform strategy, with an emphasis on state control, reduction of imports and self-sufficiency in energy. Since 1994, the state-controlled media have repeatedly proclaimed the success of this "Uzbekistan Economic Model"<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> and suggested that it is a unique example of a smooth transition to the market economy while avoiding shock, pauperism and stagnation. As of 2019, Uzbekistan's economy is one of the most diversified in Central Asia what makes the country an attractive economic partner for China.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The gradualist reform strategy has involved postponing significant macroeconomic and structural reforms. The state in the hands of the <a href="/wiki/New_class" title="New class">bureaucracy</a> has remained a dominant influence in the economy. Corruption permeates the society and grows more rampant over time: Uzbekistan's 2005 <a href="/wiki/Corruption_Perception_Index" class="mw-redirect" title="Corruption Perception Index">Corruption Perception Index</a> was 137 out of 159 countries, whereas in 2007 Uzbekistan was 175th out of 179 countries. A February 2006 report on the country by the <a href="/wiki/International_Crisis_Group" title="International Crisis Group">International Crisis Group</a> suggests that revenues earned from key exports, especially cotton, gold, corn and increasingly gas, are distributed among a very small circle of the ruling elite, with little or no benefit for the populace at large.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The early-2010s high-profile corruption scandals involving government contracts and large international companies, notably <a href="/wiki/Telecom_corruption_scandal" title="Telecom corruption scandal">TeliaSonera</a>, have shown that businesses are particularly vulnerable to corruption when operating in Uzbekistan.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the <a href="/wiki/Economist_Intelligence_Unit" title="Economist Intelligence Unit">Economist Intelligence Unit</a>, "the government is hostile to allowing the development of an independent private sector, over which it would have no control".<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The economic policies have repelled foreign investment, which is the lowest per capita in the CIS.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> For years, the largest barrier to foreign companies entering the Uzbekistan market has been the difficulty of converting currency. In 2003 the government accepted the obligations of Article VIII under the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> (IMF)<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> providing for full currency convertibility. However, strict currency controls and the tightening of borders have lessened the effect of this measure.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG/220px-Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG/330px-Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG/440px-Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1067" data-file-height="800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Urgut_Sunday_market_bread_sellers.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Bread sellers in <a href="/wiki/Urgut" title="Urgut">Urgut</a></div></div></div>
<p>Uzbekistan experienced rampant <a href="/wiki/Inflation" title="Inflation">inflation</a> of around 1000% per year immediately after independence (1992–1994). Stabilisation efforts implemented with guidance from the IMF<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> paid off. The inflation rates were brought down to 50% in 1997 and then to 22% in 2002. Since 2003 annual inflation rates averaged less than 10%.<sup id="cite_ref-imf_118-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Tight economic policies in 2004 resulted in a drastic reduction of inflation to 3.8% (although alternative estimates based on the price of a true <a href="/wiki/Market_basket" title="Market basket">market basket</a> put it at 15%).<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> The inflation rates moved up to 6.9% in 2006 and 7.6% in 2007 but have remained in the single-digit range.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government of Uzbekistan restricts foreign imports in many ways, including high import duties. Excise taxes are applied in a highly discriminatory manner to protect locally produced goods,<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> although the excises taxes were removed for foreign cars in 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> Official tariffs are combined with unofficial, discriminatory charges resulting in total charges amounting to as much as 100 to 150% of the actual value of the product, making imported products virtually unaffordable.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Import_substitution" class="mw-redirect" title="Import substitution">Import substitution</a> is an officially declared policy and the government proudly reports a reduction by a factor of two in the volume of consumer goods imported. A number of CIS countries are officially exempt from Uzbekistan import duties. Uzbekistan has a Bilateral Investment Treaty with fifty other countries.<sup id="cite_ref-bitUZ_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bitUZ-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Tashkent_Stock_Exchange" title="Tashkent Stock Exchange">Republican Stock Exchange</a> (RSE) opened in 1994. The stocks of all Uzbek joint stock companies (around 1,250) are traded on RSE. The number of listed companies as of January 2013 exceeds 110. Securities market volume reached 2 trillion in 2012, and the number is rapidly growing due to the rising interest by companies of attracting necessary resources through the capital market. According to Central Depository as of January 2013 par value of outstanding shares of Uzbek emitters exceeded nine trillion.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Thanks in part to the recovery of world market prices of gold and cotton (the country's key export commodities), expanded natural gas and some manufacturing exports, and increasing labour migrant transfers, the current account turned into a large surplus (between 9% and 11% of GDP from 2003 to 2005). In 2018, foreign exchange reserves, including gold, totalled around US$25 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Foreign exchange reserves amounted in 2010 to US$13 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Uzbekistan is predicted to be one of the fastest-growing economies in the world (top 26) in future decades, according to a survey by global bank HSBC.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Uzbekistan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Uzbekistan" title="Demographics of Uzbekistan">Demographics of Uzbekistan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Uzbeks" title="Uzbeks">Uzbeks</a></div>
<table class="wikitable" style="float: right; margin-left: 10px">
<tbody><tr>
<th colspan="4" style="text-align:center; background:#cfb;">Population<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>

</td></tr>
